First, let them debate amongst themselves, spreading despair. Then divide and sow discord, isolating them completely. Only then could they be more effectively converted.

As for the outcome?

Zhou Zhang remained unfazed.

Failure was acceptable, success merely a bonus.

If conversion failed, these captives could still serve as nourishment for the deity, they were basically food.

If conversion succeeded, they would become allies.

Even if the necromancers' conversion succeeded, Zhou Zhang dared not entertain thoughts of infiltrating the undead orthodoxy's inner circle. With her strength and standing, she knew far more than the average person and understood the undead orthodoxy possessed formidable methods. Countless factions worldwide had attempted infiltration, yet success remained exceedingly rare.

Zhou Zhang harbored a quiet apprehension toward the undead orthodoxy led by You Shen.

Had Charming Blue Goddess awakened, Zhou Zhang might have had some leverage. But with the Charming Blue Goddess slumbering for years, Zhou Zhang preferred not to provoke them.

Despite her earlier bravado, she remained acutely aware of the stakes.

However, if the human pirates were to convert, it might be possible to infiltrate their ranks.

The Fire Beard Pirates and the undead orthodoxy were both legend level superpowers. Yet they differed markedly. Compared to the undead orthodoxy's rigorous standards, the Fire Beard Pirates had a low entry threshold with minimal background checks. Anyone Fire Beard deemed acceptable, or who was recommended by his confidants, could generally join.

This recruitment policy had made the Fire Beard Pirates the largest pirate crew on the Main Plane. The combined membership of the other two legend level pirate crews didn't even reach one-fifth of Fire Beard's numbers.

“Lord Jian Gui...” Xiao Wang Zi's voice was hoarse in the underwater shack.

Jian Gui looked at him. Though Xiao Wang Zi had become a skeleton, Jian Gui could still “see” helplessness and a desperate plea for aid in his features.

Of all the undead present, Xiao Wang Zi relied on him the most.

The primary reason was his mental state. When Xiao Wang Zi became a skeleton, he was very young. Though he was now silver level, this signified only raw power. He lacked experience, his mind still immature. Moreover, he'd developed a habit of relying on others, an undeniably massive weakness.

Second was Qi Ling. Summoned by Jian Gui and serving as his alchemy assistant for years, she'd cultivated obedience. Jian Gui's decision to grant her freedom and allow her to return to aid the Da Ji Clan was a stroke of genius.

Everything unfolded exactly as Jian Gui had anticipated. As a necromancer, Qi Ling was unwelcome among the Da Ji Clan, who even ambushed her. Jian Gui intervened to aid Qi Ling on multiple occasions, earning her profound gratitude and dissolving much of Qi Ling's feelings towards the Da Ji Clan.

Next came Ku Feng. He had outright rejected Jian Gui's summons from the start; his true pursuit lay in the blade path.

Perhaps in life, Ku Feng harbored multiple desires and tangled longings. But after undergoing the baptism of death, he was forged like scrap iron, leaving only the single-minded obsession with the blade path.

Even Jian Gui's debt of gratitude to Ku Feng paled beneath this obsession.

Obsession was crucial.

Recent research by the undead orthodoxy had revealed that the key factor influencing the summoning of ghosts, skeletons, zombies, and other undead entities was this very obsession.

For ordinary beings after death, the stronger the obsession within their soul, the higher the probability of a successful summoning.

The last one was Cang Xu. He was an outsider who had survived the battle at the Underworld River, yet their time together remained brief.

Jian Gui gifted him a silver level body, one naturally equipped with hidden mechanisms. During Cang Xu's unconsciousness, Jian Gui had also tampered with his spirit. Without these measures, Jian Gui would never have trusted him as deeply as he did now.

Compared to the other three undead, Jian Gui actually admired Cang Xu the most.

“He's a talent!”

Especially after Cang Xu's earlier blackmail attempt, which only deepened Jian Gui's admiration.

The reason was simple - Cang Xu's life and death had always been within Jian Gui's grasp.

Without this layer of control, Jian Gui would undoubtedly harbor nothing but malice toward Cang Xu, seeking to eliminate him at all costs.

Watching Zhou Zhang's departing figure, Cang Xu sighed inwardly: “Any scheme or plot pales in the face of absolute power, it will be crushed effortlessly."

This sense of helplessness was all too familiar to Cang Xu.

Throughout his years, he had endured countless similar experiences.

This was a world belonging to the strong.

The foundation of power lay in the bloodline.

Wisdom was merely an embellishment. Without the backing of strength, it could only appear hollow and feeble.

"Deceptive Disguise spell proved reliable. I'm only iron level yet disguised as silver level. Though captured by Zhou Zhang, she didn't see through it. Had she, my ‘treatment’ would likely be far harsher than this."

Cang Xu swiftly scanned his surroundings, noting he was among the calmest present.

For he still had a way out.

"Zhou Zhang's intentions aren't hard to guess. For her proselytizing, forcing us to convert is perfectly normal."

“No matter which undead she chooses, she's guaranteed to win.”

“This is a severe test of Jian Gui's leadership. It could very well shatter this little undead clique!”

Cang Xu finally fixed his gaze on Jian Gui.

The atmosphere inside the shack was heavy and stifling.

Whoever was chosen as the first would face an extremely high probability of death.

The reason was simple - time was too short!

Whether converting faith or fabricating memories, both required time.

The more time, the better.

Though all were undead beings, they weren't burdened by negative energy corrosion and retained normal cognitive abilities.

Their obsessions fueled an intense will to survive. Perhaps this was why obsession became the most critical factor in summoning success.

“Jian Gui is currently the safest.”

“If we were to choose someone, his strength, past favors, and the immense influence shaped by his experiences would prevent him from being targeted.”

"Moreover, Zhou Zhang would place the gold level individuals last. If the gold level converts, the Charming Blue Church stands to gain the most."

"Their gold level strength gives Jian Gui and Ba Qi greater leverage to be courted!"

"But precisely because of this, Jian Gui's influence will be drastically reduced..."

Cang Xu pondered deeply: “Apart from Jian Gui, the others have roughly equal chances of being chosen.”

“Because none possess irreplaceable value.”

As this thought formed, Cang Xu suddenly sensed the others' gazes subtly shifting toward him.

A realization struck him: “No, the most likely candidate to be chosen... is me!”

The reason was obvious.

Cang Xu was an outsider.

Though they'd fought together in the Underworld River, compared to the time spent with Xiao Wang Zi, Qi Ling, and Ku Feng, Cang Xu was the least familiar among them.

Outsider and insider were relative concepts.

Compared to the others, Cang Xu was undoubtedly an outsider.

“Being chosen as the first to be teleported... isn't so bad either!”

After communicating with the fishman youngster, Cang Xu already knew the Deep Sea Monster Fish was hidden within the Ocean Mother Nest.

“Meeting up with the commander early means I can start crafting the memory crystal making device sooner.”

"This might be a good opportunity. I've been struggling to find an excuse to ask Jian Gui for the intermediate inheritance." Cang Xu's mindset was positive, even tinged with a hint of anticipation.

But at that moment, Jian Gui spoke, “We're all on the same side.”

“Sacrificing any one of us is not what I want to see!”

His gaze slowly swept around the group.

Xiao Wang Zi, Qi Ling, and Ku Feng, all caught in his stare, were deeply moved.

Jian Gui had long bestowed favors upon them, frequently coming to their aid. His words carried such weight that none of them doubted his sincerity for a moment.

Jian Gui's final gaze settled on Cang Xu's face: "Compared to the others, Xu Jia spent the least time with us. But after the battle in the Underworld River, I've come to accept him. He's one of us, just like the rest."

Cang Xu flinched in surprise, then his face flooded with emotion.

Jian Gui's tone grew grim: “It's all because we were too weak, we fell into Zhou Zhang's hands!”

Resentment flared across their faces.

Yes, the true villain who had them turn against each other, the one who sowed discord was Zhou Zhang!

Jian Gui shifted his tone: “We must bury this hatred deep within us and survive at all costs. Only then might we find the chance to avenge our comrades!”

Silence fell over the group.

The situation forced their hand. No matter how furious they were, would hating Zhou Zhang change anything?

Reality was brutal. The strong set the rules, and they had no choice but to play Zhou Zhang's game.

Jian Gui took a deep breath: “Therefore, I have a proposal for this selection.”

All ears perked up immediately.

Jian Gui stated: Whoever lags furthest behind in fabricating their memories would be the first candidate sent to the teleportation blood sacrifice.

After hearing this selection method, the group exchanged glances. None voiced objection.

Forging memories was their only escape route, but the workload was immense. Only through division of labor and collective effort could they succeed.

Whoever lagged behind in forging progress was the least contributor to the group. Sacrificing them would be justified. For the others, eliminating the worst performer was far more acceptable than eliminating those who had contributed more.

“It seems Jian Gui is also an excellent leader,” Cang Xu remarked, gaining new respect for him.

Though hypocritical and secretly laying traps in others, Jian Gui had demonstrated remarkable political acumen in this crisis.

Not only had he defused Zhou Zhang's provocation and calmed the group, but he had also turned external pressure into leverage, accelerating their escape plan.

Chapter 672: Qi Ling in Action

Jian Gui exerted every effort to defuse Zhou Zhang's attempts at provocation and division.

For a moment, the necromancers fell silent.

They all agreed with Jian Gui's proposed nomination rules, reaching a mutual consensus. A strong sense of urgency welled up within each of them.

Except for Cang Xu.

Qi Ling suddenly broke the silence, posing a crucial question: "Lord Jian Gui, an important matter just occurred to me. Since we're calculating based on the progress of fabricated memories, does that include the results we've already achieved in forging memories?"

This was indeed a vital question, immediately capturing everyone's attention.

Earlier, Jian Gui had taught them the method for fabricating memories, and the undead had already made progress. Xiao Wang Zi had even complained about its difficulty.

In the undead's current environment, fabricating memories was indeed arduous, even Jian Gui required considerable time.

Based on previous progress, Qi Ling ranked first, Xiao Wang Zi second, Ku Feng third, and Cang Xu fourth. Jian Gui's progress naturally surpassed everyone else, but he wasn't included in the rankings.

No one ever counted him in.

Qi Ling's question deeply concerned her own interests, interests so vital they touched on life and death, leaving her no choice but to ask.

Jian Gui paused slightly, then turned his gaze to Cang Xu and Ku Feng.

His reaction remained sharp, instantly realizing that his answer to this question would affect these two the most.

Cang Xu and Ku Feng lagged the most in progress.

Yet Ku Feng was a fighter, while Cang Xu was a necromancer, their abilities to forge memories differed fundamentally.

Clearly, fighters were vastly weaker than magicians in this regard.

In truth, ordinary fighters were nearly powerless against memory forgery. Ku Feng, as a fighter who mastered stillness training, had already tapped into his own spiritual power, granting him some ability to reshape his soul using the methods of the blasphemous priest.

But this reshaping ability was undoubtedly weaker than Cang Xu's. This was beyond doubt!

"If we include the first round's results, Xu Jia is the worst. This answer is most detrimental to Xu Jia."

"If we don't include it, then Ku Feng will likely be the first to be nominated. His progress will be clearly surpassed by Xu Jia. After all, he's merely a fighter!"

For a moment, Jian Gui felt as if he'd been placed over a fire to roast.

Choose Xu Jia, or Ku Feng?

Whoever Jian Gui chose, the other would resent him.

Thus, even a leader like Jian Gui couldn't help but show hesitation.

After pondering, he finally spoke: “It'll count.”

This answer clearly disadvantaged Cang Xu, but Jian Gui had his own calculations.

If excluded, Ku Feng would undoubtedly be the first to go. What reaction would he have in the grip of despair?

This was something Jian Gui found difficult to gauge.

True colors showed in life-and-death moments.

It was possible Ku Feng might compromise, betraying the group's blasphemy plan to Zhou Zhang in exchange for survival.

Jian Gui was confident he could eliminate Ku Feng before any secrets were revealed, using the hidden trap he'd planted on him.

But such a death, if exposed, would inevitably trigger Zhou Zhang's vigilance and investigation. This would severely disrupt Jian Gui's escape plan, potentially causing catastrophic damage.

On the other hand, Jian Gui must also consider the perspectives of Xiao Wang Zi, Qi Ling, and others. If he refused, it would inevitably lead Xiao Wang Zi and the others to believe Jian Gui valued the new over the old, to see him as cold-hearted, standing by while Ku Feng was the first to die.

Jian Gui still wished to be remembered by others as possessing warmth, not as utterly ruthless.

As Cang Xu recognized, Jian Gui was an exceptional leader, possessing keen insight into human nature.

Upon hearing Jian Gui's response, Ku Feng remained expressionless. Yet upon closer observation, one could detect a slight easing in his demeanor.

The situation was most unfavorable to him, but he had always been a steadfast warrior, unafraid of death.

What truly satisfied Ku Feng was the attitude Jian Gui had revealed. If it didn't count, it meant Jian Gui had effectively abandoned him.

That would deeply wound Ku Feng's feelings.

All along, Ku Feng had harbored strong positive emotions toward Jian Gui like admiration, respect, and reverence.

Jian Gui's expression darkened.

He realized: despite giving his all to hold the line, he couldn't reverse the damage caused by Zhou Zhang's instigation.

Zhou Zhang's overwhelming power had effortlessly disrupted the necromancers with a single phrase, nearly shattering Jian Gui's entire plan.

This was still while Zhou Zhang was in the dark about the true intentions of Jian Gui and the others.

Once she found out, the consequences would be unimaginable!

“Based on my understanding of Ku Feng, he wouldn't betray others to save himself. He'd likely rise to the challenge, unafraid of this trial.”

“Even so, Ku Feng is still the most likely candidate to be nominated first,” Jian Gui speculated silently.

After all, Cang Xu was a necromancer who had primarily cultivated the soul aspect.

If Ku Feng possessed bloodline enhancements, his ability to forge memories might surpass Cang Xu's.

But Jian Gui knew Ku Feng's situation inside out, fully aware of the latter's true bloodline.

"Compared to Ku Feng, Xu Jia might still have potential through his bloodline. After all, his bloodline mutation hasn't reached its final outcome yet," Jian Gui thought to himself.

Though he had his own predictions about the outcome of the first nomination, Jian Gui kept silent, revealing nothing.

As the superior, he offered a word or two of encouragement to everyone.

Under the shadow of death, the undead were all highly motivated, their eagerness palpable.

Cang Xu was no exception, at least outwardly, he appeared no different from the others.

Under these circumstances, he wisely abandoned his plan to request the intermediate soul aspect inheritance from Jian Gui.

Such an action would inevitably arouse suspicion - while we race against time to forge memories, you're preoccupied with intermediate soul aspect inheritance? Do you have such leisurely inclinations that you fear death? Do you have some other plan to save yourself? Are you plotting something else?

“There's no rush.” Cang Xu returned to his makeshift shelter, already seeing the right moment lurking in the near future.

Qi Ling, however, did not return to her temporary quarters. Instead, she took other action.

She visited Xiao Wang Zi alone.

Xiao Wang Zi greeted her coldly: “Hmph. What do you want?”

In his eyes, their captivity stemmed entirely from their attempt to rescue Qi Ling.

Qi Ling hastily apologized, her tone sincere as she acknowledged her mistake. She spoke of her devotion to the tribe, comparing it to the bond between Xiao Wang Zi and his parents.

These words softened Xiao Wang Zi's tone.

His obsession centered on his parents. Every year, he eagerly anticipated the day he would reunite with them.

"What good is apologizing now! You'd be better off using this time to forge memories," Xiao Wang Zi said, both angry and resigned.

Qi Ling declared: “I will compensate you and the others for this matter in the future.”

"My compensation will strive to satisfy you as much as possible. But unfortunately, by then, Ku Feng will likely be gone." Qi Ling said meaningfully.

“What do you mean?” Xiao Wang Zi didn't grasp it immediately.

Qi Ling sighed deeply with a gloomy expression: "Don't you understand? The first one selected will most likely be Ku Feng. Don't forget, he's merely a fighter."

Xiao Wang Zi froze: "But judging by current results, shouldn't Xu Jia be the most vulnerable? He's in last place; Ku Feng is stronger than him."

Qi Ling shook her head: "Xu Jia's lagging behind likely stems from his unfamiliarity with soul modification. After all, he only obtained the elementary soul aspect inheritance on Weeping Pine Island."

“As he gains more practice, his efficiency will surely surpass Ku Feng's.”

“Sigh, just thinking that Ku Feng became the first sacrifice because I dragged him down fills my heart with unease and guilt!”

“In a way, he'll die because of me!”

Xiao Wang Zi fell silent for a moment, opening his mouth to comfort Qi Ling: "Ku Feng practices stillness cultivation. He can tap into his own spiritual power. Plus, he has the advantage of starting first. He might not lose. Don't forget, this special blood sacrifice will happen three days later at most."

“Truthfully, if Xu Jia is chosen, I will be sad too.”

With Qi Ling absent, Xiao Wang Zi and Cang Xu spent every waking moment together, and Xiao Wang Zi held the latter in high regard.

Qi Ling’s tone turned cold: “Then, Xiao Wang Zi, if you had to choose between Ku Feng and Xu Jia, who would you pick to be the first?”

“This...” Xiao Wang Zi hesitated, unable to speak, then snapped, “What exactly are you trying to say?”

“I want to help Ku Feng!” Qi Ling declared her purpose.

"Compared to Xu Jia, I'd much rather not see my old friend Ku Feng die. That's why I sought you out this time, so we can act together, secretly aiding Ku Feng, helping him transform his soul."

“This...” Xiao Wang Zi lowered his head in thought.

His emotional leanings still favored Ku Feng.

Though he got along well with Cang Xu, their time together had been too brief.

"Do you really want to watch Ku Feng die? I recall several times when Ku Feng helped you." Qi Ling pressed Xiao Wang Zi for a decision.

His voice dry, Xiao Wang Zi finally spoke with overwhelming gravity: “How can we help?”

In that instant, confronting fate's cruelty and making his choice, he grew.

Zong Ge and Zi Di felt the chill too, yet their hearts burned fiercely.

“Jia Bing's alchemy lab is right here!”

Snow still drifted through the sky as they left Rabbit Fur Town behind and arrived at Grafting Town.

“Hardly expected it to be practically in the town center,” Zong Ge remarked, puzzled.

Most alchemy labs were typically situated in sparsely populated areas. On one hand, this ensured the lab's security. On the other hand, if an alchemy experiment went awry, it would minimize casualties among the surrounding population.

Yet Jia Bing's alchemy chamber was nestled within the town itself, surrounded by tightly packed houses and bustling streets.

“I'm eager to see the alchemy legacy of a gold level magician,” Zi Di said, her eyes sparkling.

Chapter 673: Jia Bing’s Alchemy Laboratory

Zong Ge and Zi Di stood side by side before Jia Bing’s residence.

Plop—!

The thunderous splash drew their attention.

As they turned their gaze, a colossal fountain shot skyward, crowned by a gigantic lotus blossom at its peak.

The lotus flower spanned an area of four acres. After being lifted into the air by the fountain, it descended again with the water column.

“Fountain King Lotus.” Information about this gold level magic plant flashed simultaneously in Zong Ge and Zi Di's minds.

The Fountain King Lotus could move by spouting water.

It currently wasn’t its seeding season. Each summer, the Fountain King Lotus would shoot up five to six hundred meters high, scattering countless bulbs made up of miniature lotus leaves.

These small leaves would swirl and dance through the air. Thousands of green lotuses radiating outward from the town center formed a unique spectacle of Grafting Town.

The Fountain King Lotus wasn't the only gold level magic plant in the town; four others exist besides it.

From where Zi Di and Zong Ge stood, they could see most of the Green Umbrella Tree's trunk, leaves, and treetop, part of the Golden Star Vine cascading down the northern cliff face, and the roof of the Pepper Tower.

Graft Town originated from the legendary Fan Sheng Za Zhong, who planted numerous magic plants in his heyday. Five of these plants still thrive today.

They were the Fountain King Lotus, the Green Umbrella Tree, the Withered Claw Tree, the Pepper Tower, and the Golden Star Vine.

The Fountain King Lotus's spouting water caught Zong Ge and Zi Di's attention, but soon their gaze shifted back to the door of Jia Bing's shop.

Many passersby glanced at Zi Di yet paid Zong Ge no mind.

To facilitate their mission, Zi Di had cast an additional Deceptive Disguise spell, transforming herself into Jia Bing's appearance. Zong Ge, meanwhile, maintained a state of “complete invisibility.”

The Deceptive Disguise spell proved remarkably effective!

Thus, for this operation, Zi Di and Zong Ge, prioritizing caution, had unanimously chosen this spell after deliberation.

To conceal their identities, they departed from Rabbit Fur Town on foot, bypassing the teleportation formation, and made their way to Grafting Town.

Only the two of them traveled alone; the rest used the teleportation formation to go directly to Snow Rabbit City.

Unburdened by others, Zi Di and Zong Ge pushed forward at full speed, covering the distance in no time.

Zi Di took a few steps forward, positioning herself before the house's main entrance. She retrieved an alchemy ring and aligned it with the keyhole.

This identity-marking alchemy ring, of course, was spoils taken from Jia Bing's corpse.

Click.

A soft sound echoed as the door swung open.

Zong Ge entered first, Zi Di following behind, smoothly slipping inside.

A row of coat hooks lined the wall in the entryway. Most hooks were bare, but three held two trench coats and hats.

Instead of entering immediately, Zi Di retrieved numerous scouting puppets from her storage device.

Then, she released several rat eye ghosts and snake shadow ghosts.

These puppets and ghosts surrounded Zi Di and Zong Ge, holding their positions without rushing into action.

Zi Di retrieved a spell scroll, tore it open, and unleashed the concealment spell within. This spell masked all movement within a certain radius, rendering it undetectable from outside.

Raising her silver level staff, Zi Di cast Illumination.

A brilliant white light radiated from the staff's tip, illuminating the surroundings.

Zi Di and Zong Ge could now see the entire interior.

The living room had been converted into a large alchemy laboratory.

Two long, brown wooden tables leaned against the grayish-white walls. The surfaces were cluttered with numerous beakers, round-bottom flasks, narrow-necked bottles, long-necked bottles, and tiny glass tubes.

In one corner of the living room stood a round, redwood barrel. Six metal pipes protruded from its top and sides, extending up to the ceiling, down through the floor, and connecting to sinks embedded in the tabletops of the long wooden tables.

Within one of these metal sinks lay a pair of green grass leaves and a small pile of silvery-yellow metal.

Gurgle... gurgle...

On the long wooden table, a glass flask contained a reagent undergoing a reaction, emitting a faint acidic odor.

“Is this... a corrosive agent?” Zi Di recognized this low level potion.

"Jia Bing left in a hurry. This much is evident from the unprocessed alchemy materials in the basin and this flask of corrosive agent is still reacting."

“He likely intended to concoct a high level corrosive acid potion.”

"He was conducting alchemy experiments when he received the Axe Gang leader's invitation. In his estimation, dealing with us wouldn't take long, just enough time for a round trip."

According to his plan, he could resume his potion brewing upon returning here.

Little did he know the final outcome would be his death, never to return to his alchemy laboratory.

Zi Di felt a slight sense of strangeness.

She now possessed considerable intelligence on Jia Bing. In her view, Jia Bing was a magician skilled in crafting alchemy puppets.

Yet here, the room contained almost exclusively tools for potion brewing.

“It seems he didn't have any alchemy assistants either.” Zi Di scanned the area several times but found no self-moving alchemy puppets.

Typically, alchemists would have several alchemy assistants.

For instance, Jian Gui had once assigned Qi Ling to serve as his alchemy assistant for many years.

Alchemy workloads are typically immense, requiring assistants to handle the grunt work and alleviate pressure, allowing the alchemist's true abilities to shine.

Zi Di had assumed Jia Bing would have one or two alchemy puppets serving as his assistants, it was his specialty, after all.

Most alchemists either train apprentices as assistants or purchase specialized alchemy puppets.

In this regard, the highest level assistants were tower spirits.

War Merchant's alchemy assistant was a tower spirit.

Not every magician possesses their own mage tower, as its construction is prohibitively expensive. Even a prosperous city like Snow Bird Harbor boasts only a single mage tower.

Zi Di analyzed: it was highly improbable that Jia Bing's alchemy assistant was a tower spirit. This was merely a rented house, not a mage tower.

Spell - Detect Traps.

Zi Di's spell revealed every trap within the house.

Some were mechanical traps, others magic.

Their number was small, their power limited.

The highest level was a gold level trap. When an enemy infiltrated, it would activate, immediately alerting Jia Bing.

This trap had already been triggered, yet no sound had been heard on the surface.

Neither Zong Ge nor Zi Di had noticed it beforehand.

“Jia Bing is dead. Even if the warning trap were triggered, it wouldn't matter.”

“But this further validates my earlier suspicion - Jia Bing's involvement in this ambush was hasty, almost an impromptu operation.”

The strength of traps lay in their concealment. Once discovered, their threat level plummeted.

Zi Di began casting spells to disarm the traps.

After several rounds of continuous casting, she successfully neutralized most of them.

The remaining traps were either insignificant, easier to destroy outright, or could be bypassed without concern.

However, several traps occupied critical positions, forcing Zi Di to resort to spell scrolls.

She had brought two high level spell scrolls for disarming traps, originally as a precaution. Using them here proved perfectly timely.

“Two scrolls still aren't enough. I'll need to stockpile more in the future,” Zi Di sighed helplessly, gazing at the final trap.

"Fortunately, this magic trap requires energy to function. Only with sufficient power supply can it unleash its immense power. If we can locate the alchemy room's energy source and cut it off at the root, we might be able to neutralize it."

Zi Di wasn't skilled in this type of magic, nor was she an exceptional thief; disarming traps was a stretch for her. This was especially true given that Jia Bing's defensive capabilities were weaker than most alchemists'.

Spell - Detect Magic.

The next moment, Zi Di's vision flooded with a multitude of magic lights.

The vast majority of magic artifacts were light sources.

Zi Di looked down and spotted the largest source of light. The magic glow was so bright that even the wooden floor couldn't block it.

“An acid-powered energy reactor?” Zi Di quickly identified the specific model.

This civilian level alchemy energy reactor supplied the laboratory with stable, continuous power.

“Huh?” Zi Di frowned in confusion.

She realized this reactor wasn't connected to the final stubborn trap.

“This means the lab has another energy source!”

"I knew it. This alchemy chamber is far too modest for a gold level alchemist."

Chapter 674: Spatial Interlayer

With this hypothesis in mind, Zi Di immediately cast a spell to detect hidden chambers.

The result revealed nothing.

“If it's not a hidden chamber, could it be an alternate dimension?”

Shortly after, Zi Di successfully cast a spell to detect alternate dimensions.

The first casting detected nothing.

“My bloodline isn't spatial type, so this spell's detection range is limited.”

“Logically, if there were an alternate space lab, where would I have placed it?”

Zi Di mentally treated herself as Jia Bing, a gold level magician, and considered the situation from that perspective.

Her gaze gradually shifted upward, fixating on the second floor.

Jia Bing's residence was a two-story building, yet when Zi Di had cast her detection spells earlier, she hadn't sensed any magic glow on the upper level.

The second floor lacked even the most basic mechanisms or magic traps.

Zi Di had assumed the second floor was merely a simple bedroom, but now it seemed riddled with suspicious elements.

She first dispatched reconnaissance alchemy puppets and ghosts to probe the area.

Finding no triggered traps, Zi Di ascended the wooden staircase to the second floor.

Of course, Zong Ge always led the way ahead of Zi Di.

Compared to the first floor, the second floor felt considerably smaller. This, however, made it easier for Zi Di to cast her spell to detect anomalies in space.

After casting it this time, an unusual spatial fluctuation instantly appeared above the bed in the bedroom.

Though subtle, the disturbance was unmistakable, even Zong Ge sensed it instantly.

“This is a spatial interlayer!”

"Who'd have thought Jia Bing's ambitions ran this deep? This interlayer is clearly man-made. Setting up something like this must have cost a fortune."

Zi Di's eyes sparkled with excitement.

She speculated, “This must be Jia Bing's primary alchemy laboratory.”

"The one on the first floor is too rudimentary, primarily used for processing herbs and brewing potions. Jia Bing's true expertise lies in crafting alchemy puppets."

“If he's willing to incur such high costs to establish this spatial interlayer, the lab inside must be exceptionally well-equipped!”

Zong Ge cut straight to the point, “So how do we get in?”

“Um...” Zi Di froze, a look of difficulty crossing her face.

She realized the spell scrolls she carried were ill-suited for this situation.

“I don't know any spatial magic myself. Even if I had a scroll to breach space, I couldn't control the force involved.”

"The slightest excess force would destroy everything inside. It could even trigger the collapse of the spatial interlayer, causing all items within to vanish along with the shattered dimensions!"

This was precisely why many magicians created such spatial interlayers - they were nearly impossible to breach. The slightest misstep could cause the space to shatter, obliterating everything within.

“We lack a powerful thief,” Zi Di sighed.

Zong Ge shook his head slightly: "Even an ordinary thief would struggle to breach a spatial interlayer. At minimum, one needs a spatial bloodline. Such thieves are exceedingly rare."

Zi Di analyzed: "This two-story cottage's defenses aren't formidable. The traps are few and hardly lethal. The ice was likely used to conceal the spatial interlayer. The layer itself is probably enough to make him confident."

"So, are we leaving empty-handed? Or should we stay a few more days and try to crack the spatial interlayer?" Zong Ge asked.

He knew little about magic and could only consult the experts.

Truthfully, Zi Di wasn't a specialist either.

Though she was a magician, her expertise lay in commerce. The realm of magic was vast and boundless, and she only excelled in potion brewing. Even so, constrained by her former bloodline, her research hadn't been particularly deep.

Now, when it came to space, it was almost entirely her blind spot.

Zi Di pursed her lips slightly.

This feeling of finding a treasure trove yet being unable to enter it was utterly miserable.

However, lingering here any longer was absolutely inadvisable.

For the survivors, the true priority remained the redemption plan.

Time was pressing.

Beyond that, they had to account for the mysterious hostile faction to which the Axe Gang leader and his crew belonged.

Even after sacrificing three gold level experts, they might still send reinforcements.

The primary reason, however, was Zi Di. She had no confidence whatsoever in cracking the spatial interlayer, nor could she even estimate how long it would take.

“Spatial-type spell scrolls are also relatively scarce on the market.”

“Perhaps contacting the Li Jian Clan could allow us to purchase spatial-type spell scrolls from them.”

The Li Jian Clan's bloodline is that of the Inner World.

It was a rather classic spatial lineage.

Consequently, the Li Jian Clan produces many spatial magicians.

"But even with these spatial spell scrolls, success isn't guaranteed. To be precise, the success rate remains quite low."

"The truly reliable approach is to have a spatial magician handle it personally. Only then can they control the force involved and perform precise operations."

"Wait a moment! Jia Bing isn't a spatial magician either. How did he create this spatial interlayer? What method does he use each time he enters it?"

This thought stirred something within Zi Di, prompting her to retrieve the identity ring once more.

She aimed the ring's surface toward the source of the spatial fluctuations.

The spatial ring flashed faintly, responding immediately. A notably intense spatial disturbance rippled out.

Zong Ge stood alert before Zi Di.

A flicker of joy crossed Zi Di's face before vanishing.

The spatial interlayer had only opened a crack. To fully unlock it, a password was required.

“A password?” Zi Di had no clue what code Jia Bing had set.

She found a new approach.

Zi Di attempted to summon a snake shadow ghost.

The snake shadow ghost distorted its form, compressing itself flat like a long, narrow sheet of paper.

Like a paper shadow slipping through a door crack, it directly entered the spatial interlayer.

The next moment, it smoothly slipped inside.

“Success!” Zi Di rejoiced.

This was the advantage of the new undead faction. Any other living being, even thinner than the compressed snake shadow ghost, would have been intercepted.

Through the snake shadow ghost's shared vision, Zi Di witnessed the scene within the spatial interlayer.

“So many puppets!” Zi Di's heart skipped a beat.

The spatial interlayer wasn't large, yet it held numerous alchemy puppets.

All these alchemy puppets were ice gargoyles.

None were iron or bronze level; the lowest rank was Silver.

The silver level ice gargoyles varied in form. They, shoulder to shoulder, lined the walls of the spatial interlayer, twelve in total. Each possessed distinct configurations. Some bore broad bat-like wings on their backs, others carried electric drills on their tails, while some sported more ferocious, razor-sharp claws. Some others were covered in fish-scale armor.

At the innermost part of the spatial interlayer stood the largest ice gargoyle.

A bitter chill swirled around this ice gargoyle. A faint aura of gold level power emanated from it.

Indeed, it was a gold level ice gargoyle.

But it was incomplete.

Approximately one-third of its body was not crafted yet.

Dozens of chisels floated around this ice gargoyle. On the ground lay cold-dispersing vessels resembling wine barrels.

Five such vessels encircled the gold level ice gargoyle.

They continuously emitted frigid cold.

This chill permeated the gold level ice gargoyle's form, stabilizing its state.

Before being fully refined, alchemy puppets often remained unstable. Many of their magic runes and formation inscriptions remained incomplete, preventing them from achieving equilibrium.

This gold level ice gargoyle was no exception.

The ice gargoyle was the most eye-catching presence. Zi Di finally managed to shift her attention elsewhere.

In the corner of the spatial interlayer, four tombstone-like ice cabinets stood.

Their fronts bore inscriptions in elven script specifying their model: Ice Water Restoration Chamber.

At the center of the spatial interlayer sat a hybrid energy reactor.

The outer unit was the massive Icebreaker Fang energy reactor. Inside the Icebreaker Fang energy reactor sat a smaller unit called the Ice Crusher energy reactor.

Both furnaces consumed frost-elemental crystals to generate stable energy.

The power produced here primarily fueled the spatial interlayer. Simultaneously, a hidden conduit channeled energy outward.

“This energy line must be what powers that final trap on the first floor!” Zi Di thought to herself.

Chapter 675: Five Color Palm Badge

Directly facing the gold level ice gargoyle stood a massive alchemy platform, leaning against the spatial wall.

Three round vials were casually placed on the platform, each containing a type of recovery cryogenic fluid. This was a specialized healing potion for ice gargoyles, all of silver level quality.

Beside the alchemy platform stood a bookshelf over a meter tall.

The shelves held various books, some slim, some thick.

<< The Complete Guide to Combat Puppet Circuits >>

<< Optimal Circuit Configuration · Volume III >>

<< Practical Battle Formations for Puppets >>

All these books pertained to alchemy puppets.

Beyond these, Zi Di also discovered several drafts and blueprints, likely authored by Jia Bing.

“What a bountiful haul!” Zi Di exclaimed, unable to conceal her delight after surveying the entire hidden compartment.

She felt an exquisite sense of fulfillment, as if her wishes had come true.

For some time now, she had sought to bolster her combat strength using alchemy puppets.

To that end, she had arranged for others to purchase combat puppets in Snow Bird Harbor. These puppets had been delivered to Blood Kin City via caravan, reaching Zi Di's hands.

Yet before these alchemy combat puppets saw a single battle, Zi Di had already obtained the superior ice gargoyle.

“Calm down. Solve the entry and exit problem first before claiming these spoils.” Zi Di steadied her mind and began manipulating the snake shadow ghost to probe the portal formation of the spatial interlayer.

Half an hour later, Zi Di had gained considerable insight.

Two options now lay before her: the first was to directly destroy the portal formation, the second was to dismantle it.

Each approach had its pros and cons.

After pondering, Zi Di discarded the dismantling option in favor of outright destruction.

Dismantling was cumbersome and time-consuming.

Direct destruction would take far less time and carry minimal risk.

Under Zi Di's command, the snake shadow ghost swiftly destroyed the portal formation.

Breaching it from within proved vastly easier than attacking from the outside.

Spatial fluctuations surged, expanding into an elliptical portal.

The portal's frame glowed silver white. Standing before it, Zong Ge could directly observe the spatial interlayer.

The portal remained remarkably stable.

Zi Di cast another round of detection spells at the entrance.

After several spells, she detected no traps within the spatial interlayer.

Yet she remained cautious, this was the primary laboratory of a gold level alchemist.

So Zi Di commanded her alchemy puppet and specter to sweep the spatial interlayer thoroughly.

The results confirmed it was safe.

Zong Ge stepped into the spatial interlayer first, followed by Zi Di.

Zi Di first gathered all books, blueprints, and drafts from the shelves. For magicians, knowledge was always the most precious commodity.

Next, she packed up the twelve silver level gargoyles.

Then, she turned her attention to the sole gold level ice gargoyle.

This ice gargoyle was still in the process of being crafted, making its disassembly and removal quite laborious.

After half an hour, Zi Di finally managed to stow the gold level ice gargoyle into her storage belt.

Thanks to Jia Bing's contribution, his gold level storage device.

Once the gold level ice gargoyle was inside, it occupied over half the storage belt's space.

Zi Di had no choice but to remove five of the silver level ice gargoyles she'd just stowed away.

The storage belt was now full.

“We need more storage devices!” Zi Di sighed, caught in a happy dilemma.

Spatial items were scarce on the market, primarily because demand from all parties was extremely high.

Whenever space storage equipment hit the market, it was usually snapped up instantly.

Moreover, explicit sales bans on spatial gear existed in many regions.

Silver level items were still manageable, but gold level ones almost always fell under these ban restrictions.

For the time being, Zi Di and Zong Ge found it difficult to acquire suitable storage items.

Zi Di quickly devised a solution.

After infusing numerous elemental crystals, she successfully activated the five silver level ice gargoyles.

With the aid of the Deceptive Disguise spell, the ice gargoyles operating outdoors would be nearly impossible to detect.

Zi Di began dismantling the energy reactors.

First was the larger Icebreaker Fang energy reactor, followed by the smaller Ice Crusher energy reactor. The former was silver level and the latter was gold level, both commanding hefty market prices.

Considering the future, the survivors might establish alchemy factories within the Ice Sculpture Kingdom. One could never have too many of these energy reactors.

After a full hour and a half, Zi Di finally emptied the entire spatial interlayer.

Emerging from the elliptical silver-edged spatial gate, Zi Di returned laden with spoils.

“Too bad this spatial interlayer can't be moved, I can't take it with me,” Zi Di glanced back at the spatial gate with regret.

Such hidden spaces held immense value, even more precious than a gold level ice gargoyle.

Regrettably, Zi Di and Zong Ge had no choice but to abandon it.

They couldn't linger here.

On one hand, the redemption plan demanded urgent execution. On the other hand, they still hadn't uncovered the true nature of the faction behind Axe Gang's leader, Jia Bing, and his crew.

The enemy had just lost three gold level members, a devastating blow. Would they seek frenzied revenge, or lay low for now? Zi Di and Zong Ge couldn't tell.

Five ice gargoyles, laden with bundles and sacks, led the way downstairs.

Following them, Zi Di and Zong Ge descended from the second floor.

Upon reaching the ground floor, they observed the last stubborn trap slowly dissipating.

After Jia Bing had set this magic trap, he had to constantly supply it with energy to maintain its formidable power.

Its energy source was the energy reactor within the spatial interlayer.

Now that Zi Di had removed the energy reactor, this most stubborn magic trap was also fading away.

Zi Di and Zong Ge initially paid it little mind, but as they reached for the door to leave, the last vestiges of the trap's magic vanished completely, revealing a peculiar metal door embedded in the ground.

The square metal door was embedded in the muddy ground, resembling a basement entrance.

“Wait, what is this?” Zong Ge halted first.

Zi Di grew curious: "Did Jia Bing set a magic trap on this door? Was he trying to conceal this metal door with this unique method?"

Both Zi Di and Zong Ge's gazes were drawn to it.

Soon, they noticed a pattern at the door's center.

It depicted an open palm with five fingers. Each finger bore a distinct color: red, yellow, blue, green, and white. From the bases of these five-colored fingers, strands of corresponding hues extended outward. These strands converged at the palm's center, intertwining to form the shape of a heart.

“What does this pattern mean?”

“It feels... like a badge.”

Zi Di and Zong Ge began studying it.

Unbeknownst to them, a similar metal door existed far away on Flower Skirt Island. Its portal also bore a palm-shaped emblem cluster.

Gold level magicians, Master Cai Jing and Da Bei, and the silver level Zhong Bei now stared intently at the five-fingered emblem, their gazes burning.

As they channeled their magic, five colored beams emerged from the fingertips.

The condensed beams weren't straight lines but curved strands, thicker than hair, resembling slender vines.

The multicolored beams slowly spread through the air before settling onto several potted plants.

These containers held various magic plants.

They were the fruits of the recent, all-out cultivation and nurturing efforts by Cai Jing, Da Bei, Zhong Bei, and numerous potion apprentices.

The multicolored beams plunged into these magic plants, continuously drawing nutrients from them.

The magic plants withered visibly at a rapid pace, while the multicolored beams grew increasingly bright and thicker in diameter.

Upon reaching a certain limit, the red beam retracted first, disappearing back into the palm pattern.

On the palm pattern with its fingers spread wide, one finger was fully illuminated, flashing red light rapidly.

Then, a similar scene unfolded in succession.

The blue-light finger, green-light finger, and white-light finger all ignited.

Only the yellow-light curve remained coiled around the magic plant. As it visibly swelled toward its limit, poised to retract, the magic plant suddenly collapsed.

A soft pop echoed.

The magic entity shattered into fragments.

The yellow light curve, however, had not reached its limit. Affected by the collapse of the magic plant, it disintegrated and vanished instantly.

“So close! So very close!” Zhong Bei cried, clutching his head.

Da Bei also wore a look of regret, “Yes, just a little more, and we could have passed the final test to inherit the legacy of Master Fan Sheng Za Zhong!”

Chapter 676: Shuang Tu - Hen Fa, You Hid Them in Vain

Master Cai Jing stared at the metal door before him. Though disappointment gnawed at his heart, his expression remained composed, “At least this proves our strategy was correct.”

Ever since he'd altered his excavation approach and recruited numerous potion apprentices, progress on cracking the legacy left by Fan Sheng Za Zhong had accelerated dramatically.

Master Cai Jing exhaled a breath of stale air before calmly analyzing, "Thanks to the ice-preservation techniques you provided, we kept the grafted magic plants alive, allowing them to gain temporary recognition from the inheritance door. But precisely because of this, we ultimately failed at the final step."

Fan Sheng Za Zhong's assessment required the heir to master grafting techniques and cultivate grafted magic plants that met specific standards.

Master Cai Jing had not truly met this standard. He achieved results through extensive cultivation: first performing initial grafting on a large number of magic plants, then selecting the healthiest specimens for forced maturation and secondary grafting to produce inferior magic plants.

These plants were unstable and could not survive long-term. After a certain period, they would collapse, wither, or die on their own.

Da Bei proactively employed his ice-preservation alchemy, a relatively advanced technique within the Ice Sculpture Kingdom's Alchemy Guild. To a certain extent, this technique stabilized a life form's condition.

After applying the ice-preservation alchemy, the survival period of the grafted magic plants significantly increased, and their stability markedly improved.

During the inheritance door's inspection, the vast majority withstood the test, failing only at the very last moment.

Da Bei was still pondering the cause of failure when Master Cai Jing's words suddenly sparked an epiphany. He realized: "The yellow light represents the sun, containing restrained heat. Therefore, it counteracted the ice-preservation alchemy, causing the magic plants to reach their time limit prematurely and collapse automatically!"

Da Bei frowned as he spoke.

This was troublesome.

The ice-preservation technique stabilized a life form's condition using freezing principles. This approach directly conflicted with solar rays.

“We'll likely need to explore another direction to solve the final puzzle,” said Da Bei.

Zhong Bei suddenly suggested, “I think the bloodline stabilizer should work!”

“The reason Master Fan Sheng Za Zhong's grafting and cultivation techniques are unstable and prone to collapse is because the grafted magic plants have bloodline conflicts.”

“I've already discerned that Master Fan Sheng Za Zhong's purpose in grafting is to leverage bloodline synergies, either to enhance a specific plant or create intricate collaborations between multiple plants, achieving a positive feedback loop.”

"Take grafting Life Papyrus onto Earth Rootstocks, for instance. Life Papyrus doesn't lack fertility, but it still requires sunlight. So, he grafted on Shimmering Bead Flowers, drastically reducing Life Papyrus's need for sunlight, even allowing it to survive long-term in shaded environments."

"Our grafted magic plants collapse easily precisely because of bloodline conflicts. Wouldn't the bloodline stabilizer solve this problem perfectly? "

“Good.” Da Bei nodded, his gaze filled with satisfaction as he looked at his son.

Master Cai Jing remained serene.

The bloodline stabilizer was primarily a magic potion for those experiencing bloodline awakening or mutation.

Bloodline mutations were rare, but awakenings were quite common.

Among the lower and middle classes, bloodline awakenings were infrequent, yet they occurred relatively often among the upper echelons.

In large clans like the Li Jian Clan, bloodline concentration was exceptionally high. As clan members matured, their bloodline concentration often reached a threshold that triggered awakening. (Of course, post-awakening bloodline concentrations varied widely. 100% concentration was considered the optimal outcome.)

At this stage, using bloodline stabilizer offers them significant advantages.

Master Cai Jing stated, “I do not possess the formula for bloodline stabilizers. Even if I did, refining such a high level potion would consume far too much time

Zhong Bei's expression shifted slightly. He noticed Master Cai Jing showed no hint of surprise, clearly having already considered this option.

Suddenly, Zhong Bei understood why Master Cai Jing had invited him and his son to collaborate.

The next moment, Zhong Bei proactively suggested, “Crafting high level potions often requires extensive practice and significant investment of manpower and resources to achieve a certain success rate.”

“We don't have much time now. There's no need to attempt brewing it ourselves. We can simply acquire it externally.”

“Every major family requires this potion. While most clans keep some stockpiled, it's rarely found on the open market.”

“But if we're talking about the largest reserves, our Alchemy Guild holds the most.”

“With my father and I stepping in, we can contact the guild. Given our standing, we can secure a batch of bloodline stabilizers directly from the Alchemy Guild at cost price!”

Master Cai Jing smiled at Zhong Bei's initiative. "My collaborations with your father have always been pleasant. Young man, you're sharp-witted, quite impressive. Let's proceed this way."

“Money is a minor concern. The real question is: how many units should we acquire?”

"Too few, and we risk failing to secure the inheritance. Too many, and we risk triggering an investigation by the Alchemy Guild."

At this point, Da Bei knew it was his turn to speak.

He declared, “Then I'll put my reputation on the line this time. I'll secure the maximum number of bloodline stabilizers and delay any related investigation!”

Master Cai Jing laughed heartily, finally satisfied. He addressed Da Bei: "Then I'll leave it to you. Remember, the sooner the better!"

Having attained gold level, Master Cai Jing was no ordinary figure.

Through a legendary inheritance he hadn't yet secured, he borrowed the power of Da Bei and Zhong Bei, using the father-son duo as bridges to leverage the might of the Ice Sculpture Kingdom's Alchemy Guild. This would aid him in conquering the inheritance trial.

At the same time, in the wild duel arena.

“Watch out!” The spectating Spring Boxer couldn't help but utter a low cry.

From the waiting room, he stared intently at Lan Zao battling in the arena.

The environment of the wild duel arena had transformed into a fully submerged underwater setting.

Deep blue seawater churned violently as Lan Zao darted through it like a swift fish.

His opponents in this battle were two ferocious sharks of iron level and bronze level.

Yes, two!

One iron level, one bronze level.

Being of the same species, they showed no signs of infighting. They attacked Lan Zao in tandem, one from the front, one from the rear.

Lan Zao's precarious situation had Spring Boxer and many other spectators on the edge of their seats.

Of course, those who had bet on the sharks were abnormally excited.

Just as the vicious sharks were about to pounce, Lan Zao suddenly darted away, deftly evading them. Instead, the two sharks, charging with too much momentum, couldn't dodge in time and collided with each other.

Lan Zao seized the opportunity to swing his blade fiercely.

He targeted the iron level shark primarily, carving three fresh gashes into its body.

Blood spurted from the wounds, the searing pain driving the shark into a frenzied rage, its attacks growing even more savage. The surrounding seawater churned violently, forming chaotic, fleeting eddies.

“I can do this!” Lan Zao's confidence surged.

He danced with the sharks.

He skillfully harnessed the turbulent currents churned by the shark. He used the currents to accelerate his evasive maneuvers and even leveraged them to propel his curved blade strikes.

Spectators couldn't help but cheer. Such an exhilarating battle had many engrossed, some even gesticulating wildly in the viewing room.

To many, the blood-red water obscured their vision. The sharks coordinated seamlessly, constantly circling for surprise attacks. Lan Zao seemed to be walking a tightrope on the edge of a cliff—one misstep could cost him his life.

But in Lan Zao's perception, it was an entirely different story!

He felt steady.

No matter how chaotic the surrounding sea became, it was his ally. Since his awakening, his field of vision had expanded vastly. Even what lay blind behind him, the currents stirred by a shark's attack, warned him of impending danger.

At that moment, Lan Zao felt like a child of the water, this place was like his domain. Every enemy movement was within his grasp.

"The Fluid Perception talent may not be directly offensive, but it's incredibly useful! It's incredibly practical!" Lan Zao marveled.

Amidst his heartfelt admiration, Lan Zao delivered a fatal blow to the iron level shark.

As his body crossed paths with the vicious shark, the bronze level curved blade in his hand plunged into its flesh. It sank almost entirely, leaving only the hilt protruding from the shark's body.

A bronze level curved blade couldn't easily pierce an iron level shark, but Lan Zao's previous attacks had already inflicted numerous wounds of varying depths on the iron level shark.

He aimed precisely at one of the deeper wounds, thrusting the curved blade into the shark's body and accurately piercing its internal organs.

The vicious shark lashed out frantically in its death throes.

Yet the chaotic currents it stirred only enhanced Lan Zao's mobility.

Half a minute of frenzied struggle drained the shark's last ounce of vitality.

With the iron level shark gone, the remaining bronze level shark stood no chance against Lan Zao.

It didn't take long for Lan Zao to dispatch the last one.

Lan Zao was thoroughly satisfied with the outcome of the battle.

“Possessing the ability to sense currents makes fighting in the ocean feel like cheating.”

“Most crucially, I didn't expose my Air Cannon's new alchemy projectile in this fight. That trump card remains in my hand.”

To be honest, when the battle began, Lan Zao had felt uneasy facing the two sharks.

But after this fight, it was as if he had undergone a baptism, breaking through some invisible barrier and gaining immense confidence.

His bloodline had already shattered his former physical limits.

The psychological barriers, however, required him to overcome them one by one.

“100%”

“This is the power of 100% Fish Bladder bloodline. At this concentration, the Fluid Perception talent proves astonishingly potent.” Bing Xiao watched the battle with equal parts astonishment and delight.

The human ringleader remained expressionless: "It's still thanks to Hen Fa's innate combat talent that he could elevate his strength so rapidly. Of course, his newly acquired iron level leather armor proved invaluable in this fight."

The Bayonet Gang had been quietly looking out for Lan Zao.

Thus, they were well aware of how he'd won a piece of iron leather armor through a tavern wager.

The human ringleader continued, "Hen Fa has defeated his opponent again this time, securing considerable funds. The black market on Flower Skirt Island is about to open. He'll likely acquire more gear there. He's entered a positive feedback loop, breaking free from his initial period of weakness. If we let him develop like this..."

The human ringleader trailed off, his words hanging in the air.

Bing Xiao understood the unspoken meaning and nodded slightly. "We must indeed invest while he's still vulnerable. Once he grows powerful, our efforts will yield half the results with twice the effort."

Bing Xiao himself valued Lan Zao's talent and held him in high regard.

Not long ago, he received a reply from the Axe Gang. Their background check on Hen Fa had allayed most of his suspicions.

With Lan Zao's background cleared, Bing Xiao began his recruitment efforts.

“Recruiting Hen Fa now would significantly boost our underwater combat capabilities.”

"Though the Bayonet Gang once controlled Snow Bird Harbor, we didn’t have many genuine sailors. Now our numbers are even thinner."

“Per our current plan, we'll be developing operations on Flower Skirt Island for a while. That means frequent voyages and making our living on the water.”

“Hen Fa's future is assured. If he develops smoothly, he'll undoubtedly become a silver level ringleader.”

“Another point: Hen Fa has already made a name for himself through wild duels.”

“Recruiting him would boost the Bayonet Gang's reputation, earning the favor of more people on Flower Skirt Island.”

Bing Xiao instinctively began calculating the benefits of recruiting Lan Zao.

This was all thanks to Shuang Tu's scheming.

With Shuang Tu's covert assistance, Lan Zao grew rapidly, his recruitment value skyrocketing and making Bing Xiao even more eager.

Finally, Bing Xiao uttered words that satisfied the human ringleader: “Let Xiao Tan recruit Hen Fa. He holds a favorable impression of Hen Fa, especially after these last two wild duels.”

Bing Xiao had no intention of recruiting him personally.

The old gang leader was wholly focused on cultivating Spring Boxer.

In his vision, Spring Boxer was destined to become the future leader of the Bayonet Gang.

Having Spring Boxer recruit Hen Fa would prove far more advantageous for the future!

Meanwhile, in the observation room, Shuang Tu couldn't help but smile.

Watching Lan Zao retreat, he felt secretly satisfied: “Hen Fa never used his alchemy projectiles during this fight.”

“Excellent!”

“He's already learning to conceal his true strength.”

“Too bad he doesn't realize that the wild duel has always been under our control behind the scenes.”

“He seeks to conceal his trump cards, yet how could I possibly be unaware of his true combat prowess?”

"Therefore, his next opponent will still surpass him slightly. Let him push beyond his limits under precisely calibrated pressure!"

Shuang Tu truly poured his heart into cultivating Lan Zao.

The wild duel arena on Flower Skirt Island was precisely forged by the forces backing Shuang Tu. Such a venue, welcoming and sending off guests, accommodating all manner of people, offered far broader opportunities than Shuang Tu's clinic could ever provide. How could he possibly give it up?

Thus, Lan Zao had hidden his trump card for nothing, Shuang Tu knew his current combat strength inside out.

“Lord, urgent intelligence!” Just then, a subordinate rushed in to report.

Shuang Tu glanced at the message and leapt to his feet in shock: "What? Cai Jing and Da Bei failed at the last moment?"

“They were just one step away from obtaining the inheritance?”

Shuang Tu's expression shifted dramatically.

He hadn't anticipated that Da Bei and Zhong Bei's involvement would lead to such a massive breakthrough in the entire operation.

“They also intended to purchase bloodline stabilizers from the Alchemy Guild...” The informant Shuang Tu had planted gave detailed and accurate new intelligence.

“Things have taken a turn for the worse,” Shuang Tu said grimly.

He couldn't sit still any longer.

His primary mission was to stop Cai Jing and secure this inheritance for his side.

Yet, despite his desire to intervene, he found himself powerless to do so.

Cai Jing's side had two gold level magicians and one silver level magician, while Shuang Tu's strongest combatant was himself, a mere silver level.

Charging into battle would be suicide; Shuang Tu stood no chance.

“What should I do?”

“Damn Ba Qi! Where the hell did he go?”

Even now, he hadn't uncovered Ba Qi's whereabouts.

“In that case, we must seek outside help.” Shuang Tu gritted his teeth in resolve.

“Contact Qian Xing! He's a saint level pirate, a member of the Fire Beard Pirates!”

“We'll request aid in Ba Qi's name.”

Qian Xing's location was relatively close to Flower Skirt Island.

He was also the only external force Shuang Tu could currently rely on.

His saint level strength placed him above Cai Jing's faction. It was highly likely that Qian Xing would ultimately inherit the legacy of Fan Sheng Za Zhong.

But there was no other choice.

For now, Shuang Tu could only pin his hopes on drawing in a powerful third party, igniting conflict between Qian Xing and Cai Jing's forces to create chaos.

If opportunity arose amidst the turmoil, he would seize it!

Chapter 677: Working with Jian Gui?

White Hair Trench.

Believers of the Charming Blue Church cleaned the bloodstains from the gold level altar.

Not far away, within a makeshift underwater hut constructed from coral and other materials, Cang Xu knelt in prayer.

A gold coin-shaped communication device slipped gently into his mouth as he recited his prayers.

Cang Xu fell silent, appearing to pray in silence, but in reality, he pressed the device against the root of his tongue.

The device gradually heated up, finally searing like a branding iron onto the base of his tongue, transforming into a round-edged badge

Within the badge lay the message the fishman youngster had entrusted to Cang Xu.

This was the tongue metal.

Carefully selected from the War Merchant's database by the fishman youngster, the device itself was highly covert. Enhanced with the Deceptive Disguise spell and Anti-investigation and Prophecy spell, it was virtually foolproof.

As Cang Xu scanned the message, his expression shifted slightly.

It caught him somewhat off guard. In this communication, the fishman youngster inquired whether Cang Xu would engage Jian Gui directly, seeking the most immediate cooperation.

For a moment, Cang Xu felt a surge of admiration, once again impressed by the fishman youngster's boldness.

Given their current setup, the Deep Sea Monster Fish lay within the Ocean Mother Nest. With the element of surprise on their side, the odds of ambushing Jian Gui were extremely high. Despite knowing they could subdue Jian Gui, the fishman youngster hadn't resorted to bullying the weaker party. Instead, he had rationally and calmly considered the possibility of cooperation between the two sides.

To be honest, Cang Xu hadn't even contemplated this possibility.

For a moment, Cang Xu fell deep into thought.

"Though bound by both divine and magic, Jian Gui still possesses combat strength through the advantages of the newly born undead faction. He could potentially free himself within the Ocean Mother Nest. Therefore, if we form an alliance with them, we wouldn't have to engage in combat within the Ocean Mother Nest. The risks involved would be significantly reduced."

"The prospects of collaborating with Jian Gui's faction have always been substantial. First, their undead inheritance is exceptionally potent, drawing suppression from every major power worldwide. Second, undead resources. Behind figures like Jian Gui lies the legendary faction led by You Shen, controlling the world's foremost undead resources. These are precisely what we will need in the future."

Cang Xu weighed the pros and cons, his mind growing increasingly calm.

From this proposal, Cang Xu clearly discerned the fishman youngster's attitude toward necromancers.

Unlike the overwhelming majority of the world, who harbored intense hostility and fear toward necromancers, The fishman youngster genuinely sought cooperation with the undead, treating them as equals.

Beyond this, Cang Xu felt profoundly moved. Though the youngster hadn't stated it explicitly in his message, Cang Xu understood: the commander proposed this alliance precisely because Cang Xu was currently at the mercy of Jian Gui. His very life hung in the balance, controlled by Jian Gui's whim.

In his message, the fishman youngster explicitly stated:

“Should our attempt to atone before the Empire's high command fail, our connection with the necromancers might enable us to evade the Empire's pursuit. This would buy me precious time and a chance to survive.”

This insight brought Cang Xu particular comfort!

In Cang Xu's view, the youngster from the Mystifying Monster Island era, though an excellent leader, was immature and overly idealistic.

By now, the young commander had matured significantly. As a leader, he considered a wide range of consequences. His decisions were often made with the team in mind, rather than solely based on personal beliefs and values.

The fishman youngster's words sparked a new thought in Cang Xu.

“If we collaborate with Jian Gui and others, establish connections, and later successfully negotiate with the Empire's high command to atone for our sins... we could then leverage that relationship to lure Jian Gui and his crew into an ambush, eliminate them, and claim it as a military achievement within the Empire.”

Cang Xu was already contemplating the possibility of targeting the necromancers in the future.

Though merely an iron level magician, he was now scheming against legend level factions. The reason lay in the Bloodcore, which had granted him boundless hope and confidence.

This world was riddled with severe class barriers. For many, their entire future was determined at birth.

Power dictated one's social standing and the primary factor determining power was bloodline.

Now, with the Bloodcore, survivors possessed infinite potential within their bloodlines.

Given sufficient time, resources, and proper cultivation, why should they be inferior to others?

Why couldn’t legend level factions be targeted too?

After deep contemplation, Cang Xu arrived at a conclusion: “Collaborating with Jian Gui and the others is indeed a sound strategy. But it's still too early to be completely open with Jian Gui."

Cang Xu remained wary of Jian Gui.

If Jian Gui learned of the fishman youngster and the Deep Sea Monster Fish, he might use that knowledge to blackmail them into submission. At that point, the measures he'd taken against Cang Xu would become crucial bargaining chips.

The fishman youngster and Cang Xu, concealed in the shadows, held the initiative. Once they negotiated with Jian Gui and revealed their whereabouts, they would fall into a profoundly passive position.

"Therefore, even if we are to cooperate, we must find the right moment. The timing is not ripe now. It's too early!"

“Another point: before the final showdown, it would be best to remove the hidden trap on my body.”

"I must handle this step myself to be fully assured. If I let Jian Gui remove them, I'd still harbor many doubts."

“But that time is approaching soon.”

Over a day had passed since Jian Gui announced the progress assessment.

During this period, nearly ten large-scale blood sacrifices had been conducted at the golden altar.

The gold coin device, carrying Cang Xu's instructions, quietly departed.

Soon after, Jian Gui summoned the undead once more.

The undead gathered within Jian Gui's shack.

Yet the atmosphere inside the shack felt different from before.

The crowd no longer stood united as before. Instead, hidden currents surged beneath the surface, mutual wariness and a fierce competitive spirit permeated the air.

On the surface, they exchanged insights and experiences about conversion, but in reality, Jian Gui was secretly transmitting his voice, leading the discussion as they collectively explored techniques for fabricating memories.

Driven by the pressure to survive, Jian Gui had never been more focused in his memory forgery efforts. Even the other undead, despite it being their first attempt, were giving it their absolute all.

This exchange of experience and technique proved beneficial to everyone, even Jian Gui gained considerable insight.

Yet, as the discussions deepened, Cang Xu's expression darkened.

He discovered his progress was still lagging behind everyone else!

"How is this possible? Ku Feng is actually ahead of me?"

Chapter 678: Cang Xu - Let Me Take a Good Look Before Death’s Door!

During their first exchange, Ku Feng had surpassed Cang Xu because Cang Xu harbored doubts about potential hidden dangers within Jian Gui's legacy and hadn't truly committed to cultivation. Thus, Cang Xu's progress was virtually nonexistent.

By their second exchange, Cang Xu had made significant strides, drastically narrowing the gap with Ku Feng.

Now, during their third exchange, Cang Xu was astonished to discover: the gap between him and Ku Feng had widened once more.

The noble steward instantly realized: Something fishy was going on!

He scanned the others. Xiao Wang Zi's gaze darted away, avoiding Cang Xu's eyes.

Qi Ling's expression remained calm.

Ku Feng, however, met Cang Xu's gaze without flinching, holding his stare for a full second.

He alone could never match Cang Xu.

But with Qi Ling and Xiao Wang Zi's assistance, he had once again gained the upper hand in progress.

“Though accepting others' help is unfair, at the brink of life and death, there's no other choice.”

“Truthfully, pitting a fighter like me against a magician to forge memories is inherently unfair.”

"It's a pity, Xu Jia. You were the last to join us. Though our shared experiences are rich, and I personally hold you in high regard, the time we've spent together is ultimately too brief."

Ku Feng felt a quiet regret for Cang Xu.

He harbored no confidence in his own conversion. The crucial assistance from Qi Ling and Xiao Wang Zi was his last lifeline.

He had every reason to cling to it desperately!

A single exchange of glances was all it took for Cang Xu to grasp: “I'm being targeted!”

“Someone is secretly aiding Ku Feng.”

Cang Xu deduced further: “It couldn't be Jian Gui. As the leader, based on what I know of him, he wouldn't stoop to getting personally involved.”

“As for the others... it must be Qi Ling.”

Cang Xu understood Xiao Wang Zi's nature well.

Xiao Wang Zi was rather innocent; he wouldn't resort to such scheming.

Qi Ling, however, was different.

Back at the undead gathering on Weeping Pine Island, Qi Ling's analysis of the world situation had already impressed Cang Xu.

Later, though constrained by Lady Sea Snake, Qi Ling deliberately revealed her traitor status, allowing Xiao Wang Zi and others to escape and preserving their bond. Ultimately, she waited for support from Jian Gui and the others. It could be said that within this small undead faction, Qi Ling's strategic acumen was truly exceptional.

“Zhou Zhang issued the rules, and Jian Gui devised countermeasures, forcing us to select the weakest candidate for memory forgery.”

"Analyzing from Qi Ling's perspective: she could never compete with Jian Gui. To maximize her chances of survival, she had to ensure she remained second only to Jian Gui."

“She must defeat everyone, including me.”

“In memory fabrication, Qi Ling holds a significant advantage as a soul aspect necromancer. Yet this edge isn't absolute, both Xiao Wang Zi and I pose threats to her.”

"Even Ku Feng, a fighter, masters stillness cultivation to tap into his spirit power. Thus Qi Ling dares not be the slightest bit careless."

"Xiao Wang Zi cultivates the bone aspect. In memory fabrication, he is clearly at a disadvantage compared to the soul aspect."

"As for me, I originally specialized in the soul aspect before switching to the Flying Head Spell. This legacy originates from Doctor Sui Shi, whom Qi Ling knows nothing about. It's only natural she views me as her greatest rival."

"Therefore, she will seek every means to eliminate me first. That's the most advantageous option for her!"

Cang Xu's thoughts raced like lightning, swiftly unraveling Qi Ling's intentions to their core.

Under normal circumstances, the undead ronin Ku Feng would be the first chosen.

The remaining contestants would then compete, increasing Qi Ling's pressure. Because Cang Xu and Xiao Wang Zi both remained.

But now, Qi Ling used friendship as an excuse, inciting Xiao Wang Zi to join her in aiding Ku Feng.

Ku Feng's progress surpassed Cang Xu's, making the latter the first to be selected.

Not only that, but Xiao Wang Zi's own progress was also hindered.

Qi Ling faced the same setback, yet her prior progress had already surpassed Xiao Wang Zi's. With both facing setbacks, Qi Ling actually solidified her advantage.

Her progress now ranks just below Jian Gui—second to none, above all others!

By doing this, Qi Ling could not only eliminate Cang Xu as a potential threat early on but also secure her own position.

Jian Gui appears generous on the surface but is secretly duplicitous, one face publicly, another behind closed doors. He has secretly deployed numerous means of control over others.

Qi Ling truly lived up to being summoned by him personally, having served as his alchemy assistant for quite some time. Their approaches to things were quite similar, often playing the sympathy card when scheming.

"If I'd been fighting alone, I'd be completely at a disadvantage right now. Fortunately, the commander is by my side!" Cang Xu remained composed and calm, still finding time for wistful sighs.

“So, what does Jian Gui think?” Cang Xu instinctively looked toward Jian Gui.

The next moment, he met Jian Gui's gaze, a look that was both complex and resigned.

In that instant, Cang Xu understood.

Jian Gui surely knew about Qi Ling's underhanded maneuvers below, yet he couldn't directly intervene.

Because any obstruction would make Ku Feng the first chosen one, essentially sending him to his death first.

Such an action would completely undermine the persona he'd meticulously crafted.

Though Qi Ling and Xiao Wang Zi had intervened to boost Ku Feng's progress, their actions had minimal negative impact on the overall process.

Had the impact been significant, Jian Gui would have intervened immediately.

“In that case, this is actually an opportunity!” Cang Xu's heart stirred.

After exchanging insights, the group wasted no time, each eager to seize every second to advance their progress.

The gathering concluded, and Xiao Wang Zi was the first to leave the shack. Feeling guilty toward Cang Xu, his departure carried a hint of escape.

Ku Feng passed by Cang Xu, gave him a slight nod, and murmured a quiet apology.

His straightforward demeanor earned Cang Xu's quiet approval. After all, employing some tactics to secure one's survival wasn't unreasonable.

Qi Ling remained silent throughout, as if unaware of everything.

Everyone else departed, yet Cang Xu alone stayed behind in Jian Gui's hut.

Seeing this, Jian Gui gave a bitter smile. "Xu Jia, I hope you understand my predicament. If I had stepped in to stop them, what would Ku Feng have thought?"

"Don't be too hasty now. Ku Feng is, after all, a fighter. You still have hope. Keep striving, and perhaps at the final moment, you could surpass him."

“Ah, this is all my fault!”

"I'm the one who led you all into this desperate situation. If only my strength had been greater, if only I could have seen through the divine spell and escaped with you all from Zhou Zhang's grasp..."

Jian Gui poured out his helplessness and regret to Cang Xu, emphasizing that Zhou Zhang, not himself, was the root of all this.

But Cang Xu sneered inwardly.

Cang Xu knew this performance was meant to prevent him from giving up entirely.

Faced with certain death, many would abandon effort and resign themselves to fate.

That was not what Jian Gui desired.

After all, if Cang Xu gave his all, significant progress could still be made during this time.

Jian Gui's act was compelling, but Cang Xu's response was equally compelling.

The noble butler sighed deeply, his voice hollow, tinged with the despair of facing death and the resignation of acceptance: “It seems I am the first to be chosen.”

“Truthfully, I have no complaints about this outcome or treatment. After all, you have all helped me greatly.”

“Had it not been for your intervention, Lord Jian Gui, I would have perished in the battle of the Underworld River.”

“But now, I am filled with profound regret.”
